Mostly, yes. I mean, I can do some regular art tasks without getting like that… it’s really when it becomes the “business” part, like client messages or invoices, where I feel like I’m supposed to seem professional and I don’t know if I’m doing it right.

A little, yeah. I don’t want to overstate it, because I’m still managing some things, but it has started spilling over.

Then it sort of builds until I’m upset at my kitchen table or I’ve let some normal responsibility slip, like a bill. I know those are basic things, so it’s embarrassing to admit.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, even when there isn’t a clear reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I have moments where I feel really discouraged or kind of flat, especially when I’ve avoided something and it piles up, but I wouldn’t say I’m hopeless all the time or that I don’t enjoy anything anymore. I still can enjoy things when I’m not caught up in all of that… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice yourself feeling physically restless, tense, or tired, even when you haven’t been doing much, especially when you’re thinking about work or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so… mostly when I’m thinking about the business stuff. Like invoices or client messages can make me feel really wound up, even if I’m just sitting there. And then I’ll avoid them, which makes it worse, and I end up kind of exhausted from stressing about it.\n\nI know it’s not like some huge crisis, but it gets to the point where I’m crying at the kitchen table over it or realizing I forgot something normal, like paying a bill. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden episodes where you felt intense fear or discomfort that came on quickly, maybe with symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, shaking, or feeling like you couldn’t breathe?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a sudden attack or anything. I get worked up and tense, but I wouldn’t say it comes out of nowhere with, like, not being able to breathe. At least, I haven’t really noticed that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself especially anxious in situations where you feel you’re being judged or evaluated by others, like presenting your work, sending an email, or even being observed while working?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe a little, but I don’t think it’s exactly being watched or judged in general. It’s more when I have to send client messages or deal with invoices for the art business, because then I start worrying I’m not doing it right or I’m going to seem unprofessional.\n\nI know that probably sounds small, but since I tried to make it a real freelance thing, I get really wound up about those parts and then I put them off. And then it piles up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or realizing I forgot something basic, like paying a bill. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel anxious about work responsibilities like invoices or client messages, do you find yourself avoiding those tasks altogether, even if you know they need to be done?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, I tell myself I’m just “waiting until I can focus” or that I’ll do it later when I’m less stressed, but honestly I think I avoid them.\n\nSince I tried to make my art a real freelance business, things like invoices and client messages just make me really wound up. Then I put them off, and it snowballs until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I’ve forgotten something normal, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds kind of dramatic for paperwork, but… that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid tasks like invoices or client messages, is it mainly because you’re worried about making mistakes, not meeting expectations, or being seen as unprofessional?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s pretty much it. I worry I’m going to do it wrong, or sound like I don’t know what I’m doing, especially with client messages or invoices. Since I tried to make my art into more of a real freelance business, those things just feel… bigger than they probably are.\n\nAnd then I avoid them, which I know doesn’t help. It gets to the point where I’m really wound up, sometimes crying at the kitchen table over it, or I forget normal things like paying a bill. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is, but that’s kind of what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Is the anxiety and avoidance limited mainly to situations where you feel your performance or professionalism is being judged, like sending client messages or creating invoices, rather than general work tasks?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes. I mean, I can do some regular art tasks without getting like that… it’s really when it becomes the “business” part, like client messages or invoices, where I feel like I’m supposed to seem professional and I don’t know if I’m doing it right.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, honestly. Since I tried to turn my art into a real freelance business, I keep getting wound up over those things and then avoiding them, and it gets to this embarrassing point where I’m crying at my kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. I know that sounds like a lot over invoices, but… yeah, that’s the main area.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about the freelance work responsibilities, like invoices and client messages? Has it been going on for months, or did it start more recently?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about a month, I think. Not months and months or anything. It started when I began trying to make my art more like a formal freelance business, with the invoices and client messages and all of that.\n\nI don’t know if that sounds like enough time to be concerned, but it’s been pretty noticeable for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that the stress or avoidance around these work responsibilities has started to affect other parts of your life, like your relationships, your overall productivity, or your ability to take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t want to overstate it, because I’m still managing some things, but it has started spilling over.\n\nLike, I put off replying to a client because I got so stuck on how to say it properly, and then at home I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with this art display. That’s not really like me, so it made me feel pretty embarrassed. It’s mostly affecting the freelance work and some home responsibilities, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that your stress or avoidance around work responsibilities has affected your ability to function in other important areas, like your self-care, social life, or overall confidence?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, a bit. I don’t think it’s taken over everything, but it has started affecting more than just the actual art.\n\nLike I put off replying to a client because I was so worried about getting it right, and then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display. That felt really embarrassing, because I usually try to stay on top of those things. So… I guess it’s affecting my freelance work and some home responsibilities, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Has this anxiety made it harder for you to interact with others, attend social events, or take care of yourself in daily routines, like eating or sleeping properly?\n\nPatient: Not so much with social events or eating and sleeping, I don’t think… at least not in a major way.\n\nIt’s more that it’s made me avoid the freelance stuff, like putting off a client reply because I was overthinking it. And then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display, which was embarrassing. So I guess it’s affecting responsibilities more than, um, basic self-care. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I know those are basic things, so it’s embarrassing to admit.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, even when there isn’t a clear reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I have moments where I feel really discouraged or kind of flat, especially when I’ve avoided something and it piles up, but I wouldn’t say I’m hopeless all the time or that I don’t enjoy anything anymore. I still can enjoy things when I’m not caught up in all of that… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice yourself feeling physically restless, tense, or tired, even when you haven’t been doing much, especially when you’re thinking about work or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so… mostly when I’m thinking about the business stuff. Like invoices or client messages can make me feel really wound up, even if I’m just sitting there. And then I’ll avoid them, which makes it worse, and I end up kind of exhausted from stressing about it.\n\nI know it’s not like some huge crisis, but it gets to the point where I’m crying at the kitchen table over it or realizing I forgot something normal, like paying a bill. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden episodes where you felt intense fear or discomfort that came on quickly, maybe with symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, shaking, or feeling like you couldn’t breathe?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a sudden attack or anything. I get worked up and tense, but I wouldn’t say it comes out of nowhere with, like, not being able to breathe. At least, I haven’t really noticed that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself especially anxious in situations where you feel you’re being judged or evaluated by others, like presenting your work, sending an email, or even being observed while working?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe a little, but I don’t think it’s exactly being watched or judged in general. It’s more when I have to send client messages or deal with invoices for the art business, because then I start worrying I’m not doing it right or I’m going to seem unprofessional.\n\nI know that probably sounds small, but since I tried to make it a real freelance thing, I get really wound up about those parts and then I put them off. And then it piles up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or realizing I forgot something basic, like paying a bill. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel anxious about work responsibilities like invoices or client messages, do you find yourself avoiding those tasks altogether, even if you know they need to be done?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, I tell myself I’m just “waiting until I can focus” or that I’ll do it later when I’m less stressed, but honestly I think I avoid them.\n\nSince I tried to make my art a real freelance business, things like invoices and client messages just make me really wound up. Then I put them off, and it snowballs until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I’ve forgotten something normal, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds kind of dramatic for paperwork, but… that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid tasks like invoices or client messages, is it mainly because you’re worried about making mistakes, not meeting expectations, or being seen as unprofessional?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s pretty much it. I worry I’m going to do it wrong, or sound like I don’t know what I’m doing, especially with client messages or invoices. Since I tried to make my art into more of a real freelance business, those things just feel… bigger than they probably are.\n\nAnd then I avoid them, which I know doesn’t help. It gets to the point where I’m really wound up, sometimes crying at the kitchen table over it, or I forget normal things like paying a bill. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is, but that’s kind of what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Is the anxiety and avoidance limited mainly to situations where you feel your performance or professionalism is being judged, like sending client messages or creating invoices, rather than general work tasks?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes. I mean, I can do some regular art tasks without getting like that… it’s really when it becomes the “business” part, like client messages or invoices, where I feel like I’m supposed to seem professional and I don’t know if I’m doing it right.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, honestly. Since I tried to turn my art into a real freelance business, I keep getting wound up over those things and then avoiding them, and it gets to this embarrassing point where I’m crying at my kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. I know that sounds like a lot over invoices, but… yeah, that’s the main area.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about the freelance work responsibilities, like invoices and client messages? Has it been going on for months, or did it start more recently?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about a month, I think. Not months and months or anything. It started when I began trying to make my art more like a formal freelance business, with the invoices and client messages and all of that.\n\nI don’t know if that sounds like enough time to be concerned, but it’s been pretty noticeable for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that the stress or avoidance around these work responsibilities has started to affect other parts of your life, like your relationships, your overall productivity, or your ability to take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t want to overstate it, because I’m still managing some things, but it has started spilling over.\n\nLike, I put off replying to a client because I got so stuck on how to say it properly, and then at home I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with this art display. That’s not really like me, so it made me feel pretty embarrassed. It’s mostly affecting the freelance work and some home responsibilities, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that your stress or avoidance around work responsibilities has affected your ability to function in other important areas, like your self-care, social life, or overall confidence?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, a bit. I don’t think it’s taken over everything, but it has started affecting more than just the actual art.\n\nLike I put off replying to a client because I was so worried about getting it right, and then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display. That felt really embarrassing, because I usually try to stay on top of those things. So… I guess it’s affecting my freelance work and some home responsibilities, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Has this anxiety made it harder for you to interact with others, attend social events, or take care of yourself in daily routines, like eating or sleeping properly?\n\nPatient: Not so much with social events or eating and sleeping, I don’t think… at least not in a major way.\n\nIt’s more that it’s made me avoid the freelance stuff, like putting off a client reply because I was overthinking it. And then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display, which was embarrassing. So I guess it’s affecting responsibilities more than, um, basic self-care. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your anxiety and avoidance only happen with tasks related to your freelance work responsibilities, or do they also occur with other types of tasks, even those that don’t involve being evaluated or making a mistake?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly tied to the freelance work, I think… especially the parts that make it feel like a real business, like invoices or client messages.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but since I tried to turn my art into more of an actual freelance thing, I get really wound up over those tasks and then I avoid them.     "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.22, F41.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um, hi. I guess I’m here because I’ve been having a harder time than I expected since trying to make my art into an actual freelance business. It sounds kind of silly when I say it out loud, but invoices and client messages get me really wound up, and then I avoid them, which obviously doesn’t help.\n\nSometimes it sort of snowballs until I’m sitting at my kitchen table crying over it, or I realize I’ve forgotten normal things, like paying a bill. I’m not trying to be dramatic—I just think I’m not handling it as well as I should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different things, like work deadlines, making mistakes, or everyday responsibilities, even when there isn’t an immediate problem?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, mostly around the freelance stuff. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but invoices or client messages can get me really wound up, and then I put them off. Then it sort of builds until I’m upset at my kitchen table or I’ve let some normal responsibility slip, like a bill. I know those are basic things, so it’s embarrassing to admit.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, even when there isn’t a clear reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I have moments where I feel really discouraged or kind of flat, especially when I’ve avoided something and it piles up, but I wouldn’t say I’m hopeless all the time or that I don’t enjoy anything anymore. I still can enjoy things when I’m not caught up in all of that… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice yourself feeling physically restless, tense, or tired, even when you haven’t been doing much, especially when you’re thinking about work or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so… mostly when I’m thinking about the business stuff. Like invoices or client messages can make me feel really wound up, even if I’m just sitting there. And then I’ll avoid them, which makes it worse, and I end up kind of exhausted from stressing about it.\n\nI know it’s not like some huge crisis, but it gets to the point where I’m crying at the kitchen table over it or realizing I forgot something normal, like paying a bill. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden episodes where you felt intense fear or discomfort that came on quickly, maybe with symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, shaking, or feeling like you couldn’t breathe?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a sudden attack or anything. I get worked up and tense, but I wouldn’t say it comes out of nowhere with, like, not being able to breathe. At least, I haven’t really noticed that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself especially anxious in situations where you feel you’re being judged or evaluated by others, like presenting your work, sending an email, or even being observed while working?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe a little, but I don’t think it’s exactly being watched or judged in general. It’s more when I have to send client messages or deal with invoices for the art business, because then I start worrying I’m not doing it right or I’m going to seem unprofessional.\n\nI know that probably sounds small, but since I tried to make it a real freelance thing, I get really wound up about those parts and then I put them off. And then it piles up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or realizing I forgot something basic, like paying a bill. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel anxious about work responsibilities like invoices or client messages, do you find yourself avoiding those tasks altogether, even if you know they need to be done?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, I tell myself I’m just “waiting until I can focus” or that I’ll do it later when I’m less stressed, but honestly I think I avoid them.\n\nSince I tried to make my art a real freelance business, things like invoices and client messages just make me really wound up. Then I put them off, and it snowballs until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I’ve forgotten something normal, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds kind of dramatic for paperwork, but… that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid tasks like invoices or client messages, is it mainly because you’re worried about making mistakes, not meeting expectations, or being seen as unprofessional?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s pretty much it. I worry I’m going to do it wrong, or sound like I don’t know what I’m doing, especially with client messages or invoices. Since I tried to make my art into more of a real freelance business, those things just feel… bigger than they probably are.\n\nAnd then I avoid them, which I know doesn’t help. It gets to the point where I’m really wound up, sometimes crying at the kitchen table over it, or I forget normal things like paying a bill. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is, but that’s kind of what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Is the anxiety and avoidance limited mainly to situations where you feel your performance or professionalism is being judged, like sending client messages or creating invoices, rather than general work tasks?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes. I mean, I can do some regular art tasks without getting like that… it’s really when it becomes the “business” part, like client messages or invoices, where I feel like I’m supposed to seem professional and I don’t know if I’m doing it right.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, honestly. Since I tried to turn my art into a real freelance business, I keep getting wound up over those things and then avoiding them, and it gets to this embarrassing point where I’m crying at my kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. I know that sounds like a lot over invoices, but… yeah, that’s the main area.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about the freelance work responsibilities, like invoices and client messages? Has it been going on for months, or did it start more recently?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about a month, I think. Not months and months or anything. It started when I began trying to make my art more like a formal freelance business, with the invoices and client messages and all of that.\n\nI don’t know if that sounds like enough time to be concerned, but it’s been pretty noticeable for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that the stress or avoidance around these work responsibilities has started to affect other parts of your life, like your relationships, your overall productivity, or your ability to take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t want to overstate it, because I’m still managing some things, but it has started spilling over.\n\nLike, I put off replying to a client because I got so stuck on how to say it properly, and then at home I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with this art display. That’s not really like me, so it made me feel pretty embarrassed. It’s mostly affecting the freelance work and some home responsibilities, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that your stress or avoidance around work responsibilities has affected your ability to function in other important areas, like your self-care, social life, or overall confidence?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, a bit. I don’t think it’s taken over everything, but it has started affecting more than just the actual art.\n\nLike I put off replying to a client because I was so worried about getting it right, and then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display. That felt really embarrassing, because I usually try to stay on top of those things. So… I guess it’s affecting my freelance work and some home responsibilities, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Has this anxiety made it harder for you to interact with others, attend social events, or take care of yourself in daily routines, like eating or sleeping properly?\n\nPatient: Not so much with social events or eating and sleeping, I don’t think… at least not in a major way.\n\nIt’s more that it’s made me avoid the freelance stuff, like putting off a client reply because I was overthinking it. And then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display, which was embarrassing. So I guess it’s affecting responsibilities more than, um, basic self-care. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your anxiety and avoidance only happen with tasks related to your freelance work responsibilities, or do they also occur with other types of tasks, even those that don’t involve being evaluated or making a mistake?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly tied to the freelance work, I think… especially the parts that make it feel like a real business, like invoices or client messages.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but since I tried to turn my art into more of an actual freelance thing, I get really wound up over those tasks and then I avoid them. And then it sort of snowballs — like I’ll end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo… I guess it starts with the work responsibilities, but it can spill into regular life a little. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel anxious or avoid tasks that aren’t related to your freelance work, like routine things around the house or simple decisions, even when no one is evaluating you or when making a mistake wouldn’t really matter?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it really happens much with totally unrelated things, no… or at least that’s not what I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s mostly since I tried to make my art into a real freelance business. I get really wound up about invoices or client messages, and then I avoid them, and it builds up until I’m, you know, crying at the kitchen table or I forget something normal like paying a bill. So it can spill over a little, but it usually starts with the freelance stuff. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Does that make sense?\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed any anxiety or avoidance around tasks that aren’t related to your freelance work, like making small decisions at home, routine errands, or other everyday activities where there’s no real consequence for mistakes?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / not_in_workforce / associates\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response\nmanifestation: \"I came in because ever since I tried to turn my art into a real freelance business, I keep getting wound up over invoices and client messages, then I avoid them until I'm crying at my kitchen table or forgetting normal stuff like paying a bill.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really as its own thing, no. I mean, I can make dinner or run an errand without getting stuck in the same way.

It’s more… since I tried to make my art into an actual freelance business, I get really wound up about the invoices and client messages, and then I avoid them. And if I avoid them long enough, then I’m crying at my kitchen table or I forget normal stuff, like paying a bill.

So it’s not usually the everyday little tasks themselves, if that makes sense. It kind of starts with the freelance responsibilities and then spills over.

Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any anxiety or avoidance around tasks that aren’t related to your freelance work, like making small decisions at home, routine errands, or other everyday activities where there’s no real consequence for mistakes?\n\nPatient: Not really as its own thing, no. I mean, I can make dinner or run an errand without getting stuck in the same way.\n\nIt’s more… since I tried to make my art into an actual freelance business, I get really wound up about the invoices and client messages, and then I avoid them. And if I avoid them long enough, then I’m crying at my kitchen table or I forget normal stuff, like paying a bill.\n\nSo it’s not usually the everyday little tasks themselves, if that makes sense. It kind of starts with the freelance responsibilities and then spills over.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever feel anxious or avoid tasks outside of your freelance work, even small ones with no real consequences, like choosing what to wear or organizing your workspace, or does this kind of reaction only happen when you’re dealing with your freelance responsibilities?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think it’s really only that kind of reaction when it’s tied to the freelance responsibilities.

Like, choosing clothes or tidying up my space doesn’t usually make me freeze or avoid it in the same way. It’s more that since I tried to turn my art into an actual freelance business, the invoices and client messages get me really wound up, and then I put them off… and if I let it build, I end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill.

So… it can affect other stuff after that, but it doesn’t usually start with the small everyday things. Is that clear?

Since I tried to turn my art into a real freelance business, I keep getting wound up over those things and then avoiding them, and it gets to this embarrassing point where I’m crying at my kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. I still can enjoy things when I’m not caught up in all of that… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice yourself feeling physically restless, tense, or tired, even when you haven’t been doing much, especially when you’re thinking about work or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so… mostly when I’m thinking about the business stuff. Like invoices or client messages can make me feel really wound up, even if I’m just sitting there. And then I’ll avoid them, which makes it worse, and I end up kind of exhausted from stressing about it.\n\nI know it’s not like some huge crisis, but it gets to the point where I’m crying at the kitchen table over it or realizing I forgot something normal, like paying a bill. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden episodes where you felt intense fear or discomfort that came on quickly, maybe with symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, shaking, or feeling like you couldn’t breathe?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a sudden attack or anything. I get worked up and tense, but I wouldn’t say it comes out of nowhere with, like, not being able to breathe. At least, I haven’t really noticed that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself especially anxious in situations where you feel you’re being judged or evaluated by others, like presenting your work, sending an email, or even being observed while working?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe a little, but I don’t think it’s exactly being watched or judged in general. It’s more when I have to send client messages or deal with invoices for the art business, because then I start worrying I’m not doing it right or I’m going to seem unprofessional.\n\nI know that probably sounds small, but since I tried to make it a real freelance thing, I get really wound up about those parts and then I put them off. And then it piles up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or realizing I forgot something basic, like paying a bill. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel anxious about work responsibilities like invoices or client messages, do you find yourself avoiding those tasks altogether, even if you know they need to be done?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, I tell myself I’m just “waiting until I can focus” or that I’ll do it later when I’m less stressed, but honestly I think I avoid them.\n\nSince I tried to make my art a real freelance business, things like invoices and client messages just make me really wound up. Then I put them off, and it snowballs until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I’ve forgotten something normal, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds kind of dramatic for paperwork, but… that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid tasks like invoices or client messages, is it mainly because you’re worried about making mistakes, not meeting expectations, or being seen as unprofessional?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s pretty much it. I worry I’m going to do it wrong, or sound like I don’t know what I’m doing, especially with client messages or invoices. Since I tried to make my art into more of a real freelance business, those things just feel… bigger than they probably are.\n\nAnd then I avoid them, which I know doesn’t help. It gets to the point where I’m really wound up, sometimes crying at the kitchen table over it, or I forget normal things like paying a bill. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is, but that’s kind of what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Is the anxiety and avoidance limited mainly to situations where you feel your performance or professionalism is being judged, like sending client messages or creating invoices, rather than general work tasks?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes. I mean, I can do some regular art tasks without getting like that… it’s really when it becomes the “business” part, like client messages or invoices, where I feel like I’m supposed to seem professional and I don’t know if I’m doing it right.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, honestly. Since I tried to turn my art into a real freelance business, I keep getting wound up over those things and then avoiding them, and it gets to this embarrassing point where I’m crying at my kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. I know that sounds like a lot over invoices, but… yeah, that’s the main area.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about the freelance work responsibilities, like invoices and client messages? Has it been going on for months, or did it start more recently?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about a month, I think. Not months and months or anything. It started when I began trying to make my art more like a formal freelance business, with the invoices and client messages and all of that.\n\nI don’t know if that sounds like enough time to be concerned, but it’s been pretty noticeable for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that the stress or avoidance around these work responsibilities has started to affect other parts of your life, like your relationships, your overall productivity, or your ability to take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t want to overstate it, because I’m still managing some things, but it has started spilling over.\n\nLike, I put off replying to a client because I got so stuck on how to say it properly, and then at home I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with this art display. That’s not really like me, so it made me feel pretty embarrassed. It’s mostly affecting the freelance work and some home responsibilities, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that your stress or avoidance around work responsibilities has affected your ability to function in other important areas, like your self-care, social life, or overall confidence?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, a bit. I don’t think it’s taken over everything, but it has started affecting more than just the actual art.\n\nLike I put off replying to a client because I was so worried about getting it right, and then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display. That felt really embarrassing, because I usually try to stay on top of those things. So… I guess it’s affecting my freelance work and some home responsibilities, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Has this anxiety made it harder for you to interact with others, attend social events, or take care of yourself in daily routines, like eating or sleeping properly?\n\nPatient: Not so much with social events or eating and sleeping, I don’t think… at least not in a major way.\n\nIt’s more that it’s made me avoid the freelance stuff, like putting off a client reply because I was overthinking it. And then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display, which was embarrassing. So I guess it’s affecting responsibilities more than, um, basic self-care. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your anxiety and avoidance only happen with tasks related to your freelance work responsibilities, or do they also occur with other types of tasks, even those that don’t involve being evaluated or making a mistake?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly tied to the freelance work, I think… especially the parts that make it feel like a real business, like invoices or client messages.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but since I tried to turn my art into more of an actual freelance thing, I get really wound up over those tasks and then I avoid them. And then it sort of snowballs — like I’ll end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo… I guess it starts with the work responsibilities, but it can spill into regular life a little. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel anxious or avoid tasks that aren’t related to your freelance work, like routine things around the house or simple decisions, even when no one is evaluating you or when making a mistake wouldn’t really matter?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it really happens much with totally unrelated things, no… or at least that’s not what I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s mostly since I tried to make my art into a real freelance business. I get really wound up about invoices or client messages, and then I avoid them, and it builds up until I’m, you know, crying at the kitchen table or I forget something normal like paying a bill. So it can spill over a little, but it usually starts with the freelance stuff. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any anxiety or avoidance around tasks that aren’t related to your freelance work, like making small decisions at home, routine errands, or other everyday activities where there’s no real consequence for mistakes?\n\nPatient: Not really as its own thing, no. I mean, I can make dinner or run an errand without getting stuck in the same way.\n\nIt’s more… since I tried to make my art into an actual freelance business, I get really wound up about the invoices and client messages, and then I avoid them. And if I avoid them long enough, then I’m crying at my kitchen table or I forget normal stuff, like paying a bill.\n\nSo it’s not usually the everyday little tasks themselves, if that makes sense. It kind of starts with the freelance responsibilities and then spills over.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or avoid tasks outside of your freelance work, even small ones with no real consequences, like choosing what to wear or organizing your workspace, or does this kind of reaction only happen when you’re dealing with your freelance responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I think it’s really only that kind of reaction when it’s tied to the freelance responsibilities.\n\nLike, choosing clothes or tidying up my space doesn’t usually make me freeze or avoid it in the same way. It’s more that since I tried to turn my art into an actual freelance business, the invoices and client messages get me really wound up, and then I put them off… and if I let it build, I end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo… it can affect other stuff after that, but it doesn’t usually start with the small everyday things. Is that clear?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or avoid tasks even when there’s no pressure or expectation to perform, such as during downtime or when no one is expecting anything from you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really when there’s no pressure. Like if it’s just downtime and nobody’s waiting on me, I’m usually okay.\n\nThe thing I came in about is more that ever since I tried to make my art into a real freelance business, I get really wound up about invoices or client messages, and then I avoid them. Does that make sense?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever feel anxious or avoid tasks even when there’s no risk of being judged or evaluated, like when you’re working on something alone or doing something where no one’s watching or expecting a certain outcome?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / not_in_workforce / associates\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response\nmanifestation: \"I came in because ever since I tried to turn my art into a real freelance business, I keep getting wound up over invoices and client messages, then I avoid them until I'm crying at my kitchen table or forgetting normal stuff like paying a bill.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think it happens much when there’s truly no one expecting anything, if that makes sense.

For me it’s really been tied to trying to make my art into a freelance business. Like, invoices or client messages feel loaded somehow, and I get wound up, then I avoid them… and if I let it go too long, I end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal stuff like paying a bill.

So, no, I don’t think it’s just when I’m alone doing something with no judgment. It’s more when it feels connected to the freelance responsibilities. Is that answering what you mean?

Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden episodes where you felt intense fear or discomfort that came on quickly, maybe with symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, shaking, or feeling like you couldn’t breathe?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a sudden attack or anything. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself especially anxious in situations where you feel you’re being judged or evaluated by others, like presenting your work, sending an email, or even being observed while working?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe a little, but I don’t think it’s exactly being watched or judged in general. It’s more when I have to send client messages or deal with invoices for the art business, because then I start worrying I’m not doing it right or I’m going to seem unprofessional.\n\nI know that probably sounds small, but since I tried to make it a real freelance thing, I get really wound up about those parts and then I put them off. And then it piles up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or realizing I forgot something basic, like paying a bill. I mean, I tell myself I’m just “waiting until I can focus” or that I’ll do it later when I’m less stressed, but honestly I think I avoid them.\n\nSince I tried to make my art a real freelance business, things like invoices and client messages just make me really wound up. Then I put them off, and it snowballs until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I’ve forgotten something normal, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds kind of dramatic for paperwork, but… that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid tasks like invoices or client messages, is it mainly because you’re worried about making mistakes, not meeting expectations, or being seen as unprofessional?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s pretty much it. Since I tried to make my art into more of a real freelance business, those things just feel… bigger than they probably are.\n\nAnd then I avoid them, which I know doesn’t help. It gets to the point where I’m really wound up, sometimes crying at the kitchen table over it, or I forget normal things like paying a bill. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is, but that’s kind of what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Is the anxiety and avoidance limited mainly to situations where you feel your performance or professionalism is being judged, like sending client messages or creating invoices, rather than general work tasks?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes. I mean, I can do some regular art tasks without getting like that… it’s really when it becomes the “business” part, like client messages or invoices, where I feel like I’m supposed to seem professional and I don’t know if I’m doing it right.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, honestly. Since I tried to turn my art into a real freelance business, I keep getting wound up over those things and then avoiding them, and it gets to this embarrassing point where I’m crying at my kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. I know that sounds like a lot over invoices, but… yeah, that’s the main area.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about the freelance work responsibilities, like invoices and client messages? Has it been going on for months, or did it start more recently?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about a month, I think. Not months and months or anything. It started when I began trying to make my art more like a formal freelance business, with the invoices and client messages and all of that.\n\nI don’t know if that sounds like enough time to be concerned, but it’s been pretty noticeable for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that the stress or avoidance around these work responsibilities has started to affect other parts of your life, like your relationships, your overall productivity, or your ability to take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t want to overstate it, because I’m still managing some things, but it has started spilling over.\n\nLike, I put off replying to a client because I got so stuck on how to say it properly, and then at home I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with this art display. That’s not really like me, so it made me feel pretty embarrassed. It’s mostly affecting the freelance work and some home responsibilities, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that your stress or avoidance around work responsibilities has affected your ability to function in other important areas, like your self-care, social life, or overall confidence?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, a bit. I don’t think it’s taken over everything, but it has started affecting more than just the actual art.\n\nLike I put off replying to a client because I was so worried about getting it right, and then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display. That felt really embarrassing, because I usually try to stay on top of those things. So… I guess it’s affecting my freelance work and some home responsibilities, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Has this anxiety made it harder for you to interact with others, attend social events, or take care of yourself in daily routines, like eating or sleeping properly?\n\nPatient: Not so much with social events or eating and sleeping, I don’t think… at least not in a major way.\n\nIt’s more that it’s made me avoid the freelance stuff, like putting off a client reply because I was overthinking it. And then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display, which was embarrassing. So I guess it’s affecting responsibilities more than, um, basic self-care. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your anxiety and avoidance only happen with tasks related to your freelance work responsibilities, or do they also occur with other types of tasks, even those that don’t involve being evaluated or making a mistake?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly tied to the freelance work, I think… especially the parts that make it feel like a real business, like invoices or client messages.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but since I tried to turn my art into more of an actual freelance thing, I get really wound up over those tasks and then I avoid them. And then it sort of snowballs — like I’ll end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo… I guess it starts with the work responsibilities, but it can spill into regular life a little. I get really wound up about invoices or client messages, and then I avoid them, and it builds up until I’m, you know, crying at the kitchen table or I forget something normal like paying a bill. So it can spill over a little, but it usually starts with the freelance stuff. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any anxiety or avoidance around tasks that aren’t related to your freelance work, like making small decisions at home, routine errands, or other everyday activities where there’s no real consequence for mistakes?\n\nPatient: Not really as its own thing, no. And if I avoid them long enough, then I’m crying at my kitchen table or I forget normal stuff, like paying a bill.\n\nSo it’s not usually the everyday little tasks themselves, if that makes sense. It kind of starts with the freelance responsibilities and then spills over.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or avoid tasks outside of your freelance work, even small ones with no real consequences, like choosing what to wear or organizing your workspace, or does this kind of reaction only happen when you’re dealing with your freelance responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I think it’s really only that kind of reaction when it’s tied to the freelance responsibilities.\n\nLike, choosing clothes or tidying up my space doesn’t usually make me freeze or avoid it in the same way. It’s more that since I tried to turn my art into an actual freelance business, the invoices and client messages get me really wound up, and then I put them off… and if I let it build, I end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo… it can affect other stuff after that, but it doesn’t usually start with the small everyday things. Is that clear?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or avoid tasks even when there’s no pressure or expectation to perform, such as during downtime or when no one is expecting anything from you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really when there’s no pressure. Like if it’s just downtime and nobody’s waiting on me, I’m usually okay.\n\nThe thing I came in about is more that ever since I tried to make my art into a real freelance business, I get really wound up about invoices or client messages, and then I avoid them. And then it snowballs until I’m crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal stuff, like paying a bill. I know that sounds kind of dramatic, but that’s the pattern I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: When you’re not dealing with freelance work responsibilities, do you ever find yourself anxious or avoiding tasks even in situations where there’s no real pressure or expectation, like making small choices or starting chores around the house?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in that general way. I mean, small choices or chores can be annoying, but I don’t usually get stuck on them unless I’m already stressed.\n\nIt’s really been since I tried to turn my art into a real freelance business. I get so wound up about things like invoices or client messages, and then I avoid them… and if I avoid them long enough, it kind of builds until I’m crying at my kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo I guess the short answer is no, not usually outside of that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or avoid tasks even when there’s no risk of being judged or evaluated, like when you’re working on something alone or doing something where no one’s watching or expecting a certain outcome?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it happens much when there’s truly no one expecting anything, if that makes sense.\n\nFor me it’s really been tied to trying to make my art into a freelance business. Like, invoices or client messages feel loaded somehow, and I get wound up, then I avoid them… and if I let it go too long, I end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal stuff like paying a bill.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think it’s just when I’m alone doing something with no judgment. It’s more when it feels connected to the freelance responsibilities. Is that answering what you mean?"
